** recipe in · mask out, the rename — LOCKED 11-20 (Q37) on his /"Recipe is fine."/ · /"amen. go. work this out."/; replays FIRST

Law, his words: /"paladin out is mask. runtime.mask daemon.mask mode.mask service.mask - universal. therefor the config in instance for that is called xMask."/ The form as WRITTEN in a json (references unresolved, a faculty by =id= alone, nothing folded) is a =xRecipe=; what paladin hands out is a =xMask=; =Faculty= is what a provider BUILT and runs. /"the instance is paladins subject and the thing it provides is paladin.instance … the schematics are still InstanceRecipe adn InstanceMask and the thing it provides is the instance prototype with all schematics guaranteed."/

#+BEGIN_SRC diff
// documentation/content/10-19_explanation/12_architecture/12.01_anatomy-of-an-instance.mdx:51
 4. **defaults and seats** — the root `lighthouse`, `datamap` and `hallucinators` are copied into every daemon that declares none of its own (a service takes the datamap), and every daemon and service is seated: `mountpoint/daemon_standalone/` is where its database and migrations will live, and its datamap copy is stamped with that seat and a file named for it, `standalone.viva.db`.
-5. **settle & publish** — the one `v.primitives.instance.Instance` schematic settles what the pinhole fired: a hallucinator with a blank secret goes *dormant* (off the roster, named on the instance), defaults fill the slots the recipe left out, a daemon still owing a lighthouse or a datamap is a fault by its own name, every miss becomes a sentence in `instance.faults` — never a throw, so the doctor can mount a bare recipe — and a clean slot decodes to its prototype: the string above becomes a `Url`. Then every `PUBLIC_*` var is published to the process environment, where clients can reach it. The boot edge refuses on faults (`instance/run`, and the runtime's own `run.js` below); `mount()` itself never does.
+5. **settle & publish** — the one `v.primitives.instance.InstanceMask` schematic settles what the pinhole fired: a hallucinator with a blank secret goes *dormant* (off the roster, named on the instance), defaults fill the slots the recipe left out, a daemon still owing a lighthouse or a datamap is a fault by its own name, every miss becomes a sentence in `instance.faults` — never a throw, so the doctor can mount a bare recipe — and a clean slot decodes to its prototype: the string above becomes a `Url`. Then every `PUBLIC_*` var is published to the process environment, where clients can reach it. The boot edge refuses on faults (`instance/run`, and the runtime's own `run.js` below); `mount()` itself never does.
#+END_SRC

Law: paladin defines a mask per host in its schematic and guarantees it; the host that builds a subject assigns its =.mask=; the registry returns modules and carries no mask; the runtime trusts paladin's shape blind (Q18 · Q19). The root =hallucinators= · =tiers= leave =InstanceMask= · =InstanceRecipe= in milestone 2 · 4, with their readers.

=InstanceRecipe.runtime= (=:185=) and each authored =services= entry (=:193=) gain =hallucinators: v.array(Recipe).optional()=, one key per row.

Q22: every host is built from ONE named object holding what the registry returned for it and its mask. A mode and a service hold both (=module= · =mask=); a daemon and the runtime hold =mask= alone: nothing in the registry names them, their =register= arrives from =wire(die.mask)= in their own first beat (Q10).

Q23, mechanical, every site: =die.runtime.instance.= → =paladin.instance.= (=runtime/lifecycle/1-population.js= ×11 after the =raw= entry dies · =3-integration.js:13=; both files import paladin) · the three copies =die.runtime.manifest= · =.url= · =.attach= =  die.mask.…= (=1-population.js:113-115=) die with the getters · =new Daemon({ mask, controller })= (=:164=, its =slug:= entry cut) · =Service.slug= the same getter, =new Service({ module: citizen, mask, controller })=. ** milestone 2 · faculty inheritance inside paladin — CAST 11-19 on his /"s2 amen"/; TANGLEABLE 11-20, replayed with milestone 4

Law: paladin folds every level before the runtime starts. Ledger → the runtime's mask in =recipe=; after the cast, runtime → its modes · its services · its daemons, daemon → its modes. =extend(below, above)= joins by provider (=module=): the level below keeps the key above and adds or restates faculties; a restated =id= keeps its keys and takes the new ones; a tier worn below removes the faculty wearing it above. Every host's mask then holds its final list.

Every host slot of milestone 1 reads =v.array(HallucinatorMask)= settled; authored, =Recipe= + =faculties: v.array(v.object({ id: v.string() }, { additionalProperties: true }))= (a restated ={ id, tier }= is whole only after the fold, so =unfold= sits between =cast= and =faults=). /(pass 11-20: =unfold= runs BEFORE =cast=. Measured: a mode entry restating ={ id, tier }= has no =type= until the fold, the settled =HallucinatorMask= refuses it, so the =ModeMasks= union takes neither arm and the entry faults =daemon[probe].modes[0] must match a schema in anyOf=. =awake= has already made every list an array, so the fold needs nothing the cast fills.)/

Probed on his ledger (=scratchpad/extend.probe.js=): ledger · runtime · spanish 14, a mode adding =francesca= (=tongue=) and restating ={ id: "oss-groq", tier: "actor" }= 15 in 3 masks, =oss-groq= keeps =model= · =upstream= · =tune= · =context=, her elevenlabs mask keeps the ledger's key; a provider the ledger lacks is added whole; source is not mutated. Completed at the cast, mine: =awake= on every declaring level, its mode labels by position as =1-population.js:79= · =wire= keeps =?? []= (a client's mask holds no =hallucinators=, =ghost/subjects/shell/lifecycle/1-population.js:10=) · the two schematics. Mine, he saw it: the merge of a restated =id=. Breaks at land, =migrate:= the key moves under =runtime=: =~/.viva/registry/education/instances/{italian,brazilian,spanish}/instance.viva.json= · =commons/instances/playground/instance.viva.json=. Red at land: =ledger.recipe.test.js:65= (=inherited=). Milestones 2 · 4 land TOGETHER: the runtime's acid still reads =instance.hallucinators= (=runtime/lifecycle/1-population.js:92=), and a daemon's folded list through today's =split= (=daemon/lifecycle/1-population.js:77=) would register the runtime's faculties twice.

The pass, 11-20, mine, reported:
- the schematic prose above is hunks: =HallucinatorMask= (settled: =type= · =id= required, =tier= a =Tier=) on =ModeMask= · =RuntimeMask= · =ServiceMask= · =DaemonMask= · =LedgerRecipe=; =HallucinatorRecipe= (his name, Q37; authored: =Recipe= + =faculties=, each ={ id, tier? }=), on the runtime · a service · a daemon · a mode entry; the root =hallucinators= leaves =InstanceMask= · =InstanceRecipe=, =Instance.hallucinators= leaves the prototype.
- =faculties= is REQUIRED on an authored hallucinator: an entry without it faults at mount. Every fixture that named a provider bare gains its faculties.
- =misworn='s call site (milestone 4's owed): a wrong-type tier is a fault, one sentence, at the level that DECLARED it. The cast =misworn(at, host)= gains =above=: a sentence the level above already says is dropped (keyed by the sentence: the cast drops object identity, measured). =worn(instance)= walks runtime · its modes · services · daemons · their modes; =settle= appends it to =faults=. An unknown tier name never reaches settle: the authored schematic refuses it at mount.
- the migration this forces, measured through the replayed copy against his shelf, read-only (checksums equal before · after):
  - his =~/.viva/instances/localhost=: 0 faults; runtime 14 faculties, chess · droneaid · spanish 14 each, every mode its host's count; the ledger's leftover =tiers= key is tolerated (=LedgerRecipe= is open)
  - =~/.viva/registry/education/instances/{spanish,italian,brazilian}/instance.viva.json=: each REFUSES to mount, =/ must not have additional properties= (the root =hallucinators=). An instance of these on prod does not boot on the landed code until its recipe moves the key
  - the move that mounts clean (probed on scratch copies): the root list onto the daemon's =hallucinators=, verbatim. Declaring it on =runtime= instead means restating the whole =runtime= slot: an instance inherits the ledger's runtime only while it declares none
  - what changes with it, his ruling Q2 at work: each of the three ALSO holds the ledger's providers now (spanish · brazilian gain openrouter's 8; italian holds 9)
- =commons/instances/playground/instance.viva.json= restates the ledger's =runtime= slot to carry its stub (same reason); it now holds the ledger's 14 beside its 5.
- labels: a runtime-level dormant reads =runtime.hallucinators[N]= (was =hallucinators[N]=); the doctor's rows and two qa suites follow.
- the cases written: the fold's six unit cases + ledger → runtime (=ledger.recipe.test.js=) · the unfold through settle, a worn tier, the wrong-type fault said once (=settle.test.js=) · =registry.wire.test.js= (a mode wires its own mask · the provider stamp) · the authored schematic (=instance.test.js=).

#+BEGIN_SRC js
// subsystems/paladin/gestalten/belt/faculties.js
const merge = (mask, declared, tiers) => {
  const own = declared.flatMap((held) => held.faculties);
  const restated = (faculty) => own.some((held) => held.id === faculty.id);
  const kept = mask.faculties.filter((faculty) => !tiers.has(faculty.tier) && !restated(faculty));
  const inherited = (id) => mask.faculties.find((faculty) => faculty.id === id);
  const added = own.map((faculty) => ({ ...inherited(faculty.id), ...faculty }));
  return Object.assign({}, mask, ...declared, { faculties: [...kept, ...added] });
};

export function extend(below, above) {
  const tiers = new Set(below.flatMap((mask) => mask.faculties).flatMap((faculty) => faculty.tier ?? []));
  const named = (module) => below.filter((mask) => mask.module === module);
  const fresh = below.filter((mask) => !above.some((held) => held.module === mask.module));
  return [...above.map((mask) => merge(mask, named(mask.module), tiers)), ...fresh];
}
#+END_SRC

+function unfold(instance) {

+  if (!runtime) return;
+  for (const mode of runtime.modes) mode.hallucinators = extend(mode.hallucinators, runtime.hallucinators);
+  for (const service of instance.services) service.hallucinators = extend(service.hallucinators, runtime.hallucinators);
+  for (const daemon of instance.daemons) {

+describe("faculty unfolding — extend(below, above), the one fold every level runs; ledger → runtime in the recipe beat (m126 Q2)", () => {
+  const voice = { module: "@commons/hallucinator/elevenlabs", secrets: { key: "above" }, faculties: [{ type: "speech", id: "leandro", tune: [0.3, 0.9, 0.5], tier: "tongue" }, { type: "verbatim", id: "scribe", tune: [0.3, 1.0, 0.5] }] };
+  const mind = { module: "@commons/hallucinator/openrouter", secrets: { key: "above" }, faculties: [{ type: "dialogue", id: "oss-groq", model: "llama", tune: [0.4, 1.0, 0.8] }] };
+
+  it("nothing below: the level holds what is above, the same masks", () => {
+    expect(extend([], [voice, mind])).toEqual([voice, mind]);
+  });
+
+  it("a provider named below joins by module — it keeps the key above and adds its faculty; an untagged faculty above is kept", () => {
+    const [held] = extend([{ module: voice.module, faculties: [{ type: "speech", id: "luiza", tune: [0.3, 0.8, 0.5] }] }], [voice]);
+    expect(held.secrets).toEqual({ key: "above" });
+    expect(held.faculties.map((faculty) => faculty.id)).toEqual(["leandro", "scribe", "luiza"]);
+  });
+
+  it("a restated id is ONE faculty: its keys above, the keys below over them", () => {
+    const [, held] = extend([{ module: mind.module, faculties: [{ id: "oss-groq", tier: "actor" }] }], [voice, mind]);
+    expect(held.faculties).toEqual([{ type: "dialogue", id: "oss-groq", model: "llama", tune: [0.4, 1.0, 0.8], tier: "actor" }]);
+  });
+
+  it("a tier worn below is unique in the set: the faculty wearing it above leaves, under any provider", () => {
+    const francesca = { type: "speech", id: "francesca", tune: [0.3, 0.9, 0.5], tier: "tongue" };
+    const [held, own] = extend([{ module: "@commons/hallucinator/deepgram", secrets: { key: "below" }, faculties: [francesca] }], [voice]);
+    expect(held.faculties.map((faculty) => faculty.id)).toEqual(["scribe"]);
+    expect(own).toEqual({ module: "@commons/hallucinator/deepgram", secrets: { key: "below" }, faculties: [francesca] });
+  });
+
+  it("a provider only below is added whole, after those above", () => {
+    expect(extend([mind], [voice]).map((mask) => mask.module)).toEqual([voice.module, mind.module]);
+  });
+
+  it("source is not mutated", () => {
+    const below = [{ module: mind.module, faculties: [{ id: "oss-groq", tier: "actor" }] }, { module: voice.module, faculties: [{ type: "speech", id: "francesca", tier: "tongue" }] }];
+    const above = [voice, mind];
+    const before = plain({ below, above });
+    extend(below, above);
+    expect(plain({ below, above })).toEqual(before);
+  });
+
+  it("the ledger's hallucinators are the runtime's — an inherited runtime holds them, a declared one adds to them; the root holds none", async () => {
+    const supplied = (paladin) => ledger(paladin, { hallucinators: [mind] });
+    const inherited = await mount({ ledger: supplied, instance: { daemons: [daemon] } }, { VIVA_LEDGER_ANIMA: "http://anima:1794/", VIVA_LEDGER_SERVE: "http://ledger:1/" });
+    expect(inherited.faults).toEqual([]);
+    expect(inherited.hallucinators).toBeUndefined();
+    expect(inherited.runtime.hallucinators.map((mask) => mask.faculties.map((faculty) => faculty.id))).toEqual([["oss-groq"]]);
+    expect(inherited.daemons[0].hallucinators).toEqual(inherited.runtime.hallucinators);
+    const own = { manifest: { slug: "own" }, statics: { serve: "http://own:1/", remote: "http://own:1/" }, secrets: { jwt: "${SECRET_VIVA_LEDGER_JWT}" }, hallucinators: [{ ...voice, secrets: { key: "${SECRET_VIVA_LEDGER_JWT}" } }] };
+    const declared = await mount({ ledger: supplied, instance: { daemons: [daemon], runtime: own } }, { VIVA_LEDGER_ANIMA: "http://anima:1794/" });
+    expect(declared.faults).toEqual([]);
+    expect(declared.runtime.hallucinators.map((mask) => mask.module)).toEqual([mind.module, voice.module]);
+  });
+});
+

** milestone 4 · a cortex per host — CAST 11-19 on his /"s4 amen."/; TANGLEABLE 11-20, replayed with milestone 2

Law: every host builds its OWN cortex from its own mask: =wire(mask)= → =register= → each provider called INLINE in the host's =acid= beat → =new Cortex(faculties)= on the subject (Q26: no belt). =split= · a =tiers= table in a recipe · =/metadata/tiers= die. A tier is a name in typology's table; a faculty wearing it wins on its host. Lands WITH milestone 2.

=subsystems/typology/schematics/primitives/instance.js=: =:7= imports =Tier= in place of =Tiers=; the three rows =tiers: Tiers.optional(),= (=:135 · :191 · :211=) leave; a =HallucinatorMask= faculty gains =tier: Tier.optional()=. =migrate:= the =tiers= key leaves =~/.viva/ledger.viva.json:30= · =commons/ledger/ledger.viva.json:11= (=InstanceRecipe= is closed: a recipe still holding it faults). OWED before the replay, mine: =misworn='s call site into =faults= (after the fold a branch's misworn faculty repeats on every leaf: one sentence per faculty, at the level that declared it). /(pass 11-20: done, =worn(instance)=, under milestone 2's pass and its tangle)/

Q7A, a provider carries its entry forward: ONE added row, the entry spread FIRST, in each builder (probed: a provider takes =tier= · =provider= on its entry and drops both today).

The same row: =commons/hallucinators/openrouter/provider/index.js:148= (=...model=) · =:169= · =:180= · =elevenlabs/provider/index.js:220= · =:247= (=...entry=) · =deepgram/provider/index.js:148= · =:177= · =commons/fixtures/testing/hal/stub/provider/index.js:112= · =:125= · =:149=.

He saw, amen: no =catch= (a refusing provider faults its host, a mode's its daemon; read, not run) · the mode's die key pointed at =mode.cortex= (9 spread · lift sites read it) · the mode's =consume= through its own =wire= · a service builds no cortex · =Tier= off the table's keys, a wrong-type tier a FAULT · the wire in the runtime's =registry= beat (unrun on a booted runtime). Met at the cast, mine, reported: =new Cortex(TIER_POINTS)= now THROWS (/invalid faculty/: 59 sites in 25 test files turn red, each → =new Cortex()= or =new Cortex([…])=; shown to him as green) · the three registry rigs calling =new Cortex()= turn green (known issue =registry-rigs-build-a-cortex-without-tiers=) · anima's own =TIERS= list (=sections.js:455=) keeps its five names. Red at land besides: =hello.test.js:167= · =beats.coverage.test.js:250= · =cortex.test.js:246-248= · =scenarios/bench.js:41=. The four points for =ear= · =tongue= · =actor= · =director= are GUESSES, mine.

#+BEGIN_SRC js
// systems/runtime/tests/runtime/unfold.integration.test.js
import { specimen } from "@vivalence/typology";
import { STUB_FACULTIES } from "../../../../commons/fixtures/testing/data/stub.js";
import { hello } from "../scenarios/index.js";

const { describe, it, expect, beforeAll, afterAll } = specimen;

const STUB = "@commons/hallucinator/stub";
const RECIPE = {
  ...hello.RECIPE,
  runtime: { ...hello.RECIPE.runtime, hallucinators: [{ module: STUB, faculties: STUB_FACULTIES }] },
  daemons: [
    {
      ...hello.RECIPE.daemons[0],
      modes: [
        {
          module: "@commons/demo/hello-world",
          hallucinators: [
            {
              module: STUB,
              faculties: [
                { id: "stub-speech", tier: "tongue" },
                { type: "speech", id: "stub-voice", tune: [0.9, 0.1, 0.1] },
              ],
            },
          ],
        },
      ],
    },
  ],
};

describe("faculty unfolding on a booted runtime — the recipe's hallucinators reach every host's own cortex (m126)", () => {
  let world;
  let finn;
  const served = async (host) => {
    const response = await world.get(`${host}/metadata/cortex`, finn.access);
    expect([host, response.status]).toEqual([host, 200]);
    return await response.json();
  };
  const ids = (faculties) => faculties.map((faculty) => faculty.id).sort();

  beforeAll(async () => {
    world = await hello.boot({ recipe: RECIPE, prefix: "viva-unfold-" });
    await (await world.post("/lighthouse/signup", { username: "finn", password: "hunter22" })).json();
    const login = await (await world.post("/lighthouse/login", { username: "finn", password: "hunter22" })).json();
    finn = { access: login.authority.access };
  });

  afterAll(() => world.stop());

  it("the runtime and its daemon each serve the runtime's five; no /metadata/tiers anywhere", async () => {
    const five = STUB_FACULTIES.map((faculty) => faculty.id).sort();
    expect(ids(await served("/runtime/house"))).toEqual(five);
    expect(ids(await served("/daemon/hello"))).toEqual(five);
    expect(ids(await served("/runtime/house/mode/home/probe"))).toEqual(five);
    for (const host of ["/runtime/house", "/daemon/hello"]) {
      const response = await world.get(`${host}/metadata/tiers`, finn.access);
      expect([host, response.status]).toEqual([host, 404]);
      await response.body?.cancel();
    }
  });

  it("the mode that added a faculty and restated one holds six: its own on top of its daemon's, the restated one wearing its tier, stamped with its provider", async () => {
    const own = await served("/daemon/hello/mode/demo/hello-world");
    expect(ids(own)).toEqual([...STUB_FACULTIES.map((faculty) => faculty.id), "stub-voice"].sort());
    const worn = own.find((faculty) => faculty.id === "stub-speech");
    expect([worn.type, worn.tier, worn.provider, worn.tune]).toEqual(["speech", "tongue", "stub", [0.3, 0.8, 0.9]]);
    expect((await served("/daemon/hello")).find((faculty) => faculty.id === "stub-speech").tier).toBeUndefined();
  });
});
#+END_SRC

** Q36 · a fault spells a host and a mode by its lookup path — LOCKED 11-20 on his /"b. and also for daemon"/; replays FIFTH, after milestone 4

Law: a label in a fault · a requirement · a dormant entry is the path a reader would look the thing up by: =daemons.<slug>= · =services.<slug>= · =clients.<slug>= · =<host>.modes.<type>.<slug>=. =named= (beside =identity=, =2-resolution.js=) is the one speller of a mode, =label= the one walker of a pointer; a mount-time refusal goes through the same =label= (it printed the raw pointer =/daemons/0/modes/0=). A mode paladin cannot name before =wire= (a bare file entry, Q25) keeps its position, =modes[1]=; a host without a slug its position too. =runtime= stays =runtime=; a hallucinator keeps its position (=hallucinators[1]=).

Mine, reported: his words name the daemon; =services= · =clients= take the same spelling (one grammar, the =echoed= pattern reads both). Readers of a label are opaque (prefix match: =commons/instances/hello-world/tools/doctor.js:110=, =paladin/gestalten/commands/instance/doctor.js:38=), none parses the brackets (grepped, the registry too).

Probed on the clone (=scratchpad/q36=): =daemons.probe.modes.game.board claude wears ear as dialogue= · =daemons.probe.modes[1] claude wears tongue as dialogue= (the file entry) · =instance.mount: … daemons.probe.modes.game.hello must be string= · =daemons.probe.modes.office.vdex.mountpoint=.

// subsystems/paladin/instance/lifecycle/2-resolution.js:90

+export const named = (entry, index) => {
+  const [type, slug] = identity(entry) ?? [];
+  return type ? `.${type}.${slug}` : `[${index}]`;
+};
+
+const hosted = (declaration, index) => {
+  const slug = declaration?.manifest?.slug;
+  return slug ? `.${slug}` : `[${index}]`;
+};
+
+export const label = (instance, pointer) =>
+  pointer
+    .split("/")
+    .slice(1)
+    .reduce(
+      ({ at, node, key }, part) => {
+        const next = node?.[part];
+        if (!/^\d+$/.test(part)) return { at: at ? `${at}.${part}` : part, node: next, key: part };
+        if (key === "modes") return { at: at + named(next, part), node: next, key };
+        if ([instance.daemons, instance.services, instance.clients].includes(node))
+          return { at: at + hosted(next, part), node: next, key };
+        return { at: `${at}[${part}]`, node: next, key };
+      },
+      { at: "", node: instance, key: "" },
+    ).at || "instance";
+
 const referencing = (declaration, daemon) => (entry) => {

** recipe in · mask out, every pair named — LOCKED 11-20 (Q37) on his /"why isnt there a faculty primitive?"/; replays LAST

Law, his words: /"We have faculty mask, the thing that goes out. We have faculty in … And then we just have faculty. And faculty in is the thing that lives in the ledger. And the plain faculty is the thing that actually runs inside of the system."/ =FacultyRecipe= · =FacultyMask= sit beside =Faculty= (=v.primitives.hallucination=); =instance.js= imports the two. Every in-form that sat inline in =InstanceRecipe= is a named export: =Recipe= · =HallucinatorRecipe= · =ModeRecipe= · =ModeRecipes= · =RuntimeRecipe= · =LighthouseRecipe= · =ClientRecipe= · =ServiceRecipe= · =DatamapRecipe= · =StaticsRecipe= · =SecretsRecipe= · =EnvironmentRecipe=, one key per row. Out: =StaticsMask= is a schematic now (it was a function taking the typed keys; its three typed sites say =v.object({ … }, { additionalProperties: true })= themselves) and =SecretsMask= · =EnvironmentMask= (the open object =InstanceMask.environment= held inline), all exported.

The pairs after this section, pinned by =instance.test.js= /every type holds a recipe and a mask under one name/: Instance · Runtime · Daemon · Mode · Service · Client · Lighthouse · Datamap · Hallucinator · Statics · Secrets · Environment (=xRecipe= · =xMask=) · Faculty (=FacultyRecipe= · =FacultyMask= · =Faculty=) · the generic =Recipe= · =Mask= · the ledger, a recipe ALONE (=LedgerRecipe=: nothing hands a ledger out, paladin folds it into the instance's recipe).

The ledger joins as a GUARANTEED recipe, the one behaviour this section adds: =ledger.recipe()= (=subsystems/paladin/ledger/prototype.js=) faults what it read against =LedgerRecipe= and throws, beside its manifest check. =LedgerRecipe.environment= is the =EnvironmentRecipe= now (it was an open object). Measured: =commons/ledger/ledger.viva.json= 0 faults · his =~/.viva/ledger.viva.json= ONE, =/runtime must not have additional properties= (the stray ="traits": []= at =:13=): on the landed code his ledger REFUSES until that row leaves (=* the shelf=). Emulated for his file (parts read, manifest derived by hand), the real beat run by the suite. A fixture of mine wrote a literal secret in a ledger hallucinator; it holds a reference now. The fault prints the raw pointer, as the manifest check one row above it does (Q36's =label= lives in the instance's lifecycle; not imported across).

Proved pure (=scratchpad/n.rp36= against =scratchpad/n.q37=, the JSON of each schematic hashed before · after): =InstanceRecipe= · =DaemonRecipe= · =InstanceMask= · =DaemonMask= · =ModeMask= · =ModeMasks= · =RuntimeMask= · =ServiceMask= · =ClientMask= · =HallucinatorMask= · =Mask= · =LighthouseMask= · =DatamapMask= are byte-equal.

The ONE schematic that changes: =LedgerRecipe= now holds the recipe forms (=RuntimeRecipe= · =LighthouseRecipe= · =Recipe= · =HallucinatorRecipe= · =ClientRecipe= · =ServiceRecipe=), where it held the out-forms. Behaviour-neutral, measured: its three readers take =Object.keys(….properties)= alone (=paladin/instance/lifecycle/1-population.js:28= · =paladin/gestalten/commands/ledger/doctor.js:16= · testament's frozen copy), the keys are the same eight. Before this section NOBODY validated a ledger file against it. Measured raw, both ledger files, read-only: the old out-form faulted them on many rows (=/runtime/statics/serve must be RFC 3986 URI= on a =${…}= reference · =/lighthouse/secrets/jwt must not have fewer than 24 characters= · =/runtime must have required properties hallucinators= …); the recipe form leaves =/ must have required properties manifest= · =/environment must be object= (a part path) on both, and =/runtime must not have additional properties= on his (the stray =traits=, =~/.viva/ledger.viva.json:13=).

+describe("recipe in, mask out — every pair is a named primitive (m126 Q37)", () => {

+    expect(FacultyMask.faults({ id: "leandro", tier: "tongue" })).toEqual([{ at: "/", reason: "must have required properties type" }]);
+    expect(FacultyMask.faults({ type: "speech", id: "leandro", tier: "tongue" })).toEqual([]);
+    expect(Faculty.faults({ type: "speech", id: "leandro", tier: "tongue" }).map(({ reason }) => reason)).toEqual(["must have required properties tune, via"]);
+  });
+
+  it("every type holds a recipe and a mask under one name; the ledger file is a recipe alone", () => {
+    const names = Object.keys(v.primitives.instance);
+    const TYPES = ["Instance", "Runtime", "Daemon", "Mode", "Service", "Client", "Lighthouse", "Datamap", "Hallucinator", "Statics", "Secrets", "Environment"];
+    for (const type of TYPES) {
+      expect(names).toContain(`${type}Recipe`);
+      expect(names).toContain(`${type}Mask`);
+    }
+    expect(names).toContain("Recipe");
+    expect(names).toContain("Mask");
+    expect(names).toContain("LedgerRecipe");
+    expect(names).not.toContain("LedgerMask");
+    expect(names.filter((name) => TYPES.includes(name) || name === "Ledger")).toEqual([]);
+  });
+
+  it("a hallucinator's faculties are the primitives, on both sides", () => {
+    const { FacultyRecipe, FacultyMask } = v.primitives.hallucination;
+    const { HallucinatorRecipe, HallucinatorMask } = v.primitives.instance;
+    expect(JSON.stringify(HallucinatorRecipe.properties.faculties.items)).toBe(JSON.stringify(FacultyRecipe));
+    expect(JSON.stringify(HallucinatorMask.properties.faculties.items)).toBe(JSON.stringify(FacultyMask));
+  });
+});
+
#+END_SRC

** Q43 · a mode entry holds traits at its top and no manifest (Q44) — TANGLEABLE 11-20, replays SECOND

His /"a"/ on the home of an entry's =traits=, then on the walk /"no unheld. no manifest. lets be consistent."/ and Q44 /"a."/: a mode entry is =module= · =traits= · =trait= · =mountpoint= · =statics= · =secrets= · =consume= · =hallucinators=. =manifest= is no key of =ModeRecipe= (the closed object refuses it) and no slot of =ModeMask=; the mask fills =traits: []=; the fold takes the entry's words from there.

- the fault a =manifest= on an entry gets, as the schematic prints it: =/daemons/0/modes/0 must not have additional properties=. It names the entry, never the key: =v.js='s =faults= prints no key for that keyword.
- LOST capability, his ruling: an entry can no longer override its module's manifest (=name= · =slug= · =type= · =icon= · =description=); a mode is what its module says, plus the entry's =traits=. Cases that pinned it and died: =instance.test.js= /a mode mask carries …/ (=manifest: { name: "X" }= on an entry passed) · =registry.wire.test.js= /a mask folds manifest and statics by key/ (an entry turned =board= into =board-2=; now it pins statics and the entry's traits).
- KEPT, measured: =registry.accio= still folds =query.manifest= and its =traits= for what is NOT a mode entry: a SERVICE entry carries its whole manifest (=ServiceRecipe.manifest: Manifest=; =registry.wire.test.js:141-154= pin it, =ATTACHED= + an added word). =2-resolution.js:75-76= (=identity=: an entry's own =type= + =slug= win) is untouched: unmeasured whether an inline module still reaches it; dead for a mode entry.
- the comment above =accio= (=registry.js:248=) says where an entry's traits sit now.

- the fold (=registry.accio=) lifts =traits= OUT of the mask before it spreads it over the module: a domain module exports its own =traits= (its trait sources), a spread would overwrite it.
- DIFFERS from a client, said to him: a client wears =HOME= on its MANIFEST and a top-level =traits= is refused there (=subsystems/typology/tests/schematics/instance.test.js=, /a client carries HOME on its MANIFEST/); a mode entry now mirrors the lighthouse instead.
- no recipe writes a mode entry's =manifest= (11 files: his instances · registry instances · commons instances · the ledger); repo fixtures that did, each read: =paladin/tests/registry.integrity.test.js:111= (its =traits= moved up) · =typology/tests/schematics/instance.test.js:187= (cut, now asserts the refusal) · =paladin/tests/registry.wire.test.js:38= (cut, above).

// documentation/content/10-19_explanation/12_architecture/12.03_faculty-unfolding.mdx:29

-## question one — are the AI traits inferred?
+## answered — the AI traits are a mode's own

-Two traits describe a mode's AI surface. `HARNESSED` means the mode has a model to talk to. `CONVERSATIONAL` means a human converses there: typed, dictated, called.
+Two traits describe a mode's AI surface. `HARNESS` means the mode has a model to talk to. `CONVERSATION` means a human converses there: typed, dictated, called. A thread carries their config under its own words, `HARNESSED` and `CONVERSATIONAL`, as it carries `TOOLED` and `PRIMED` for a mode's `TOOLING` and `PRIMER`.

-Today both are declared by the mode's author in `manifest.traits`, and every new thread wears both regardless. The only code that looks at the faculties is one predicate inside the `HARNESSED` trait, which adds the voice routes when an ear, a mind and a mouth are all present:
+A mode wears a trait because its module declares it in `manifest.traits`, or because its entry in a recipe adds it. Nothing is inferred and nothing travels: a runtime and a daemon wear neither.
+
+```json
+{
+  "module": "@education/game/dojo",
+  "traits": ["HARNESS", "TOOLING"],
+  "trait": {
+    "HARNESS": { "tier": "fast", "rounds": 6 },
+    "TOOLING": { "/daemon/spanish/mode/game/dojo": "eager" }
+  }
+}
+```
+
+`traits` adds to what the module declares. `trait` is optional: config for a trait the mode wears, in the shape of the thread's twin. A thread that does not wear the twin yet takes it as its starter when it is created on that mode or moved onto it; a thread that already wears it keeps its own, through every change of mode.
+
+What does travel is the faculties. Paladin checks each mode that wears `HARNESS` or `CONVERSATION` against the faculties folded down to it (ledger, runtime, daemon, mode) and reports a miss; the mode still mounts. The trait serves the routes its faculties allow, read off one table:

 ```js
-// systems/runtime/subjects/mode/traits/harnessed.js
-const streams = (type) => modeDie.cortex.find({ type, via: "stream" }).length > 0;
-
-if (streams("call") || (streams("verbatim") && streams("dialogue") && streams("speech")))
-  harness.branch("/call")
+// subsystems/typology/schematics/primitives/hallucination.js
+export const REQUIRES = {
+  HARNESS: [["dialogue"]],
+  CONVERSATION: [["call"], ["verbatim", "dialogue", "speech"]],
+};
 ```
-
-The open question is whether that predicate should become the rule. A mode would be `HARNESSED` when its cortex holds a small set of faculties (a dialogue model), and `CONVERSATIONAL` when it holds a larger set that includes the first (an ear and a mouth as well). Capability would be checked at each level of the chain and resolved at the mode.
-
-A second part of the same question: traits that *travel*. If the runtime wears `HARNESSED` or `CONVERSATIONAL`, its daemons and their modes inherit it. A daemon or a mode may still embed its own version and steer it — its language, its voices, its models. Today nothing travels: a parent hands its modes the catalog of trait functions, never the traits themselves.
-
-What inference would change for an author: a mode that declares nothing, on a daemon that holds a dialogue model, becomes harnessed.
…

-The direction for `CONVERSATIONAL` is that the trait itself carries the wiring its presence implies, on the runtime and on the client: the voice routes, the storage of spoken turns, the selection of a faculty for each job (ear, voice, the mind that speaks), and the split of a call into an *actor* that speaks and a *director* that calls tools and updates the actor's context. Today the trait on a mode is an empty function; that wiring sits inside `HARNESSED` and in typology.
+The direction for `CONVERSATION` is that the trait itself carries the wiring its presence implies, on the runtime and on the client: the voice routes, the storage of spoken turns, the selection of a faculty for each job (ear, voice, the mind that speaks), and the split of a call into an *actor* that speaks and a *director* that calls tools and updates the actor's context. Today the trait on a mode is an empty function; that wiring sits inside `HARNESS` and in typology.

// subsystems/typology/schematics/traits/overwrite.js
import { enhance, v } from "../v.js";

const bare = (schematic) => {
  const { default: filled, ...descriptors } = Object.getOwnPropertyDescriptors(schematic);
  return enhance(Object.defineProperties({}, descriptors));
};

export const overwrite = (schematic) =>
  v.object(
    Object.fromEntries(
      Object.entries(schematic.properties).map(([key, property]) => [
        key,
        ("properties" in property ? overwrite(property) : bare(property)).optional(),
      ]),
    ),
    { additionalProperties: false },
  );
#+END_SRC

#+BEGIN_SRC js
// systems/runtime/tests/thread.starter.test.js
import { specimen, Vector } from "@vivalence/typology";
import { ThreadEntity, traits } from "@vivalence/runtime";
import { create } from "./scenarios/cortex.js";

const { CONVERSATION, TOOLING, PRIMER } = traits.mode;

const STARTER = {
  HARNESS: { tier: "fast", rounds: 6 },
  CONVERSATION: { language: ["it"], polish: { engaged: false } },
  TOOLING: { "/daemon/house/mode/teacher/dewey": "eager" },
  PRIMER: { "/daemon/house/mode/teacher/dewey/voice": "lazy" },
};
const FILLED = {
  HARNESSED: { tier: "fast", effort: "low", rounds: 6, slugging: "deep" },
  CONVERSATIONAL: { language: ["it"], hear: {}, speak: {}, call: {}, polish: { engaged: false } },
  TOOLED: { "/daemon/house/mode/teacher/dewey": "eager" },
  PRIMED: { "/daemon/house/mode/teacher/dewey/voice": "lazy" },
};
const TWINS = Object.keys(FILLED);

let scenario;

specimen.describe("a thread's starter — each mode trait seeds its own twin on the mode's twitch (m126 Q40 · Q47)", () => {
  specimen.beforeAll(async () => {
    scenario = await create();
    const { dewey } = scenario;
    dewey.module.primer = new Vector();
    CONVERSATION(dewey);
    TOOLING(dewey);
    PRIMER(dewey);
    dewey.trait = STARTER;
  });

  specimen.afterAll(async () => {
    await scenario.orm.close();
  });

  const stored = (thread) => scenario.em.fork().findOneOrFail(ThreadEntity, { id: thread.id }, { filters: false });
  const twins = (thread) => Object.fromEntries(TWINS.map((twin) => [twin, thread.trait[twin]]));
  const loose = async (held = {}) => {
    const thread = scenario.em.create(ThreadEntity, { user: scenario.fixtures.user, mode: null, trait: {}, counter: 0, ...held });
    await scenario.em.flush();
    return thread;
  };

  specimen.it("born on the mode: it wears the four twins, each the mode entry's config, stored", async () => {
    const born = await stored(await scenario.createThread());
    specimen.expect(born.traits).toEqual(TWINS);
    specimen.expect(twins(born)).toEqual(FILLED);
  });

  specimen.it("born with no mode it wears none; moved onto the mode, it is seeded at the move", async () => {
    const thread = await loose();
    specimen.expect((await stored(thread)).traits).toEqual([]);
    thread.mode = scenario.dewey.entity;
    await scenario.em.flush();
    const moved = await stored(thread);
    specimen.expect(moved.traits).toEqual(TWINS);
    specimen.expect(twins(moved)).toEqual(FILLED);
  });

  specimen.it("a twin it already wears is kept through the move, the others are seeded", async () => {
    const thread = await loose({ traits: ["HARNESSED", "TOOLED"], trait: { HARNESSED: { tier: "balanced" }, TOOLED: {} } });
    thread.mode = scenario.dewey.entity;
    await scenario.em.flush();
    const moved = await stored(thread);
    specimen.expect(moved.traits).toEqual(["HARNESSED", "TOOLED", "CONVERSATIONAL", "PRIMED"]);
    specimen.expect(moved.trait.HARNESSED).toEqual({ tier: "balanced", effort: "low", rounds: 12, slugging: "deep" });
    specimen.expect(moved.trait.TOOLED).toEqual({});
    specimen.expect([moved.trait.CONVERSATIONAL, moved.trait.PRIMED]).toEqual([FILLED.CONVERSATIONAL, FILLED.PRIMED]);
  });

  specimen.it("a mode entry with no config seeds the bare word: the schematic's fill alone", async () => {
    scenario.dewey.trait = {};
    const bare = await stored(await scenario.createThread());
    scenario.dewey.trait = STARTER;
    specimen.expect(bare.traits).toEqual(TWINS);
    specimen.expect(bare.trait.HARNESSED).toEqual({ tier: "capable", effort: "low", rounds: 12, slugging: "deep" });
    specimen.expect([bare.trait.TOOLED, bare.trait.PRIMED]).toEqual([{}, {}]);
  });
});
#+END_SRC
